        - (q1) **Confirm how the gift ruling is stored.** Ruling: gifted shares are an external outflow for the monthly return. Stored as: the snapshot's `deposits_withdrawals` stays cash-only; `twr` (time-weighted return) is Modified Dietz with each gift as a dated outflow; `investment_gain` excludes gifts. So in a gift month `total − starting − deposits_withdrawals` does not equal `investment_gain`, on purpose. The alternative, putting the gift value in `deposits_withdrawals`, breaks daily cash stepping and the Dietz cross-check (`flow-total-mismatch`).

    - ~~(a) **[USER DECISION] The cloud Worker can still send a recap for an entry replaced on the Mac** after the 2 AM snapshot, when the Mac is asleep or offline. The Worker reads the replaced flag from the snapshot and does not re-check at send time. Two options: (1) the Mac writes the existing mac-sent marker for a replaced row (cheap; the marker then says "sent" for a row that was never sent, covers only rows the Mac tried to send, and lasts about 30 hours); (2) ship the replaced-entry ids to the Worker through the armed-events delta and have its scan honour them (both sides change, plus parity pins).~~ (SHIPPED 2026-10-07 sprint `074b7d4a`, `877a7489`: option 2, by the owner's ruling. The armed-events payload carries the replaced and the deleted earnings entries; the Worker marks them one way. Deploy the Worker first. The accepted consequence is an owner question in the sprint follow-ups entry.)
    - (b) **[USER DECISION] Should the later of two hand-entered earnings rows also be blocked on the two manual send buttons?** Today it is refused on the sweep and the debrief only. One line decides it: `refuseIgnoredManualTwin` in `lib/earnings/send-service.ts`.
    - (c) **[USER DECISION] Should an API-key failure count as account-level?** A rejected or missing key (HTTP 401, 403, or no key configured) currently does not use up an article's retries. To read the ruling narrowly, remove `auth` and `config` from `ACCOUNT_KINDS` in `lib/gmail/enrichment-failure.ts`.
    - (d) **[USER DECISION] The Giving lot drawer's "Suggest highest-gain" still prefers a lot with an implausibly low basis** (it ranks by gain per share). The ruling covered the row flag and the year total only.
    - (e) **[USER DECISION] Should a flagged Giving row hide its own basis and gain figures?** Today it prints them beside the flag; only the year total drops the row.

Three repair scripts remain for the owner: (g) and (h) here, plus the transcript-sections script in the Codex-branch item below)

  - **Follow-ups, none blocking**
    - (j) Enrichment: a 404 and a 409 from the provider still count toward `MAX_ENRICH_ATTEMPTS` as unknown errors. ~~The Sync Feeds status line (`app/api/research/sync/route.ts`) shows only processed and failed, so an outage does not name the AI account.~~ (SHIPPED 2026-10-07 sprint `10a7f157`: the sync response says when a pass stopped on an account-level AI failure; it does not yet say which cause, see the sprint follow-ups. The 404 and 409 counting is still open.) Not verified against the live provider: the status and type of an out-of-credit reply, and whether the AI gateway passes error bodies through unchanged. The Worker's own newsletter failure handling was not reviewed (it does not use the attempt count).
    - (k) Earnings emails: a claim for an event id with no calendar row surfaces the raw FOREIGN KEY error (pre-existing). The archive link can point at a cloud-sent email, which has no local body. The sweep books a replaced-entry refusal under the skip label "not-ready" (the message is right; a new label needs an edit in `lib/calendar/email-sweep.ts`). The viewer for a marked email still rebuilds its scoreboard from the replaced row. An email sent earlier on the later of two live hand-entered rows is not marked. The retired `wrap-send.ts` would treat the two new refusal codes as a live-claim conflict if it were revived. The two manual routes were tested at the service, not through the route handlers.

An option on a fund that has no stored sector can still land in an AI-assigned sector until the fund's own sector is filled. A fund with cached look-through weights is spread across sectors while its option sits whole in the fund's bucket (the ruling's accepted trade-off; spreading the option is the possible follow-up). A stale row on an underlying with no held option waits for the repair script.

The resolve-symbol route still runs a full recompute although its own change cannot move a lot. A long recompute over the phone tunnel can outlive the connection (the dialog says the change may have been saved). The confirm counts are raw table counts and will not equal the Tax Lots page tiles.

- [ ] **Ruled builds, Codex's branch — LANDED 2026-10-07** (transcripts: the last two high rulings; the Tax Lots page: rulings 8, 13 and the folded data-health finding). Built by Codex, reviewed by Claude (Opus); the transcript unit needed three builds. ~~**Owner steps:** run `scripts/repair-transcript-sections.ts` dry-run on a copy, read the counts, then `--apply` (it rewrites stored Guidance and Risk sections; expect nearly every stored call to change).~~ (RUN LIVE 2026-10-07 at the start of the sprint, after a backup, per the sprint log.) **Follow-ups, none blocking:** (a) **A wrong 8-K inside the four-day window is stored as the print's release and never replaced** (the only content check is the EDGAR client's loose "2.02 / Results of Operations" test; a pre-announcement or an unrelated 8-K can win): prefer a filing whose text states the expected quarter, and let a better filing replace a stored one inside the 36-hour window. (b) **The fetch button and the chat default still request a calendar quarter when the latest print has no Finnhub entry** (truthfully keyed, but an older call can come back as "the latest"). (c) **Vendor quota:** the bound is per print for 36 hours, not per day across prints; the 24-hour upgrade clock asks once a day for ten days per print, so several heavy print days can exceed the 25-a-day tier; with no filing, the two newest prints take both attempt slots (both predate this work): add a per-day counter in `settings` and order candidates by oldest attempt. **(2026-10-07 night `6fe18aa0`, PR #102, not merged: the per-day counter is built, keyed by vendor and Eastern date, bumped before each vendor call so a failed call counts. Ordering candidates by oldest attempt stays OPEN.)** (d) Parser: the earliest mention near "earnings / results / call" wins, so "guidance for the first quarter 2027 and results for the fourth quarter 2026" reads Q1 2027 (a safe rejection for a call; a wrong key for a filing with no Finnhub entry). (e) Guidance keywords miss inflected forms ("expects"); a guidance excerpt can swallow an adjacent risk sentence. (f) A legacy filing or a legacy call that states nothing, sitting at the print's fiscal key, blocks that print's own fetch. (g) `audit-transcript-keys.ts` lists date-matched filings as contradictions (stored that way by design). (h) Tax Lots: ~~the page pin asserts a string its own anchor contains; the chat realized total has no USD-only guard while the tiles do (pre-existing);~~ (SHIPPED 2026-10-07 sprint: the pin anchors on the rendered block `e33f323b`; the chat summary's realized totals use the tiles' USD-only rule and say how many sales were left out `cd3ac4e2`); the security page counts expired lots, not contracts; ~~the script that retires expired option holdings is not built.~~ (WRITTEN 2026-10-07 night `3c9a0f34`, PR #103, not merged: `scripts/retire-expired-option-holdings.ts`, dry run by default, two flags to write. Its dry run on a copy found nothing to retire. Not run on live data.) (i) Not checked on a real vendor response: that Finnhub's fiscal year and the vendor's agree for every offset-fiscal company (stored rows for one January-year-end name are consistent).

(b) **[USER DECISION] A bond fund takes market beta 1.0 in the custom scenario engine**, and the rate leg now stacks on it (rates +200bp with market -10% marks a bond fund down about a fifth). (c) **[USER DECISION] Two hand-entered rows:** a print on the later date gets no recap until the earlier row is deleted; the 14-day window chains; push at print and the digest / briefing lists still use both rows; a vendor row confirmed in place still hides two hand-entered rows. (d) **After the first real sync, check** the log for `Expected exactly one contract ... got N` and `Skipping contract lookup for ...`, that each option underlying has one `prices` row per trading day with `source='tws'`, that none appears in holdings, and that `coupon_rate` is still empty everywhere. (e) **Broker coupon source** (`lib/tws/bond-coupon.ts`) is unwired; prove a contract-details request by contract id on a real session before wiring it. (f) The rate-limited vendor scan still deletes rows for symbols it never scanned (now named in the outcome). (g) ~~`HoldingsTable.tsx` keeps an inline twin of the zero-basis predicate;~~ (SHIPPED 2026-10-07 sprint `6d06597d`, `125b7103`: one shared `hasKnownBasis` in `lib/compute/known-basis.ts`); the Gain % denominator is a signed sum, so long and short net. (h) The name parser does not block CMS-linked or CPI-linked notes. (i) An ETF the broker types as a stock gets no rate leg until the sync retypes it. (j) Not browser-checked at phone width: the moved footer block, the Hub's later-row line.

- [ ] **Follow-ups from the 2026-10-07 landing of PR #100** (four nightly fixes, three of them the 2026-10-06 high rulings: equity curve, Diagnostics window captions, pre-release actual chip; one read-only Opus review, one review fix, re-review clean, sandbox smoke 4/4, full suite 11,125 passed). None blocking: (a) **[USER DECISION] Equity curve "too incomplete to plot" rule** — a segment longer than 7 days is drawn as a captioned straight line when it has fewer than three interior daily values OR they vary by more than 30% of their mean; a month with a large deposit can trip the 30% test and be labelled incomplete, and a month with dailies on only its first days draws an uncaptioned straight run. The ruling did not define the threshold. **2026-10-07 sprint:** Claude dropped the 30% test and captioned sparse runs (`2bc0a35a`); Codex called dropping it a direction change, so the test was restored as it was (`0fc4c440`) and the captions stay. Still the owner's call (both views: `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-10-07 evening). ~~(b) The equity-curve caption describes all history, not the selected range, and says "months" for any skipped segment.~~ (SHIPPED 2026-10-07 sprint `2bc0a35a`) (c) **[USER DECISION, same question as the slot-less earnings ruling]** a hand-entered actual on a vendor row with no slot stays "pre-release" until the app's default 16:15 even when the company reported before the open. (d) The pre-release chip clears on re-render only (no timer). PARTLY SHIPPED 2026-10-07 sprint: one timer clears it on Today releases (`2bc0a35a`) and on the week view (`7be9ead1`); the Hub rows are not wired yet (see the sprint follow-ups). (e) `POST /api/calendar/events` answers 409 "already exists, edit it instead" when the colliding manual row is superseded and invisible; same class as the revive fix, not fixed. (f) The 5-calendar-day lookback for the curve's start reference is a judgment; an older prior value falls back to the first in-segment daily.

- [ ] **Overnight 2026-10-05 — open rulings and follow-ups from the backlog run** (commits `163bf9e1`…`80897e5c`; direction-only): (1) **USER DECISION — a slot-less vendor earnings row still stores the 16:15 default (the 2026-10-05 ruling is only display-ready)**: a history-derived time (the slot of the last reported print) was built and then removed in review, because for a slot-less row the accept floor, the enrichment window and the recap floor fall back to the stored time, so a single past print could open those gates hours early and the earlier-wins conflict clause would keep it; a safe version needs at least two agreeing vendor-sourced prints, or a display-only time; making it "time unknown" needs a due-time rule in seven readers (Mac `enrichment-runner`, `email-sweep`, `wire-probe`, same-day transcripts, worksheet; Worker `calendar-enrich`, `fallback-earnings`) plus the pre-print floor, print-watch's own fallback and `EarningsDateChip`. User asks, in priority order: → **SUPERSEDED for (a)(b)(d)(e) by the live print v2 design (2026-09-02 evening, Bugs/Quality section).**
  - (a) **PDF drop support** — the drop zone is `.html/.txt` only, but IR sites publish releases as PDFs; the user had the CRWD PDF in hand and the picker greyed it out. The app already parses PDFs elsewhere (Claude native document blocks — vanguard-statement importer, bogeys upload route); wire the same path into the drop-zone ingest so a PDF becomes a print-watch document.
  - (b) **Auto first-pass analysis on drop** — after (a): when a release lands (drop OR acquisition), a smart-tier model runs an unprompted "how does this look vs expectations?" pass (bogeys + guidance vs actuals, segment color). Motivation: user currently detours to ChatGPT with the PDF because they don't yet trust in-app chat for this; the pass should build that trust in-product.
  - (c) **Accept-gate slot floor** — accepting a line at 16:12 ET warned "event is in the future (17:00)": the pre-print confirm compares against the stored release time, which for AMC names is often the CALL time (the CRWD/RBRK trap — both windows had to be re-aimed live via `symbol_release_times` user overrides). Gate should use slot floors instead: AMC → accept freely after 16:00 ET, BMO → after 07:00 ET. Sibling hardening: web-verify that stores exactly 17:00 for an AMC print should be flagged as suspect call-time.
  - (d) **Learn per-company line-item shape from full releases** — persist which metrics each company actually reports (from parsed docs/PDF drops) and feed next quarter's contracts; folds into the existing accepted-lines provenance store item above.
  - (e) **Fallback bogeys always present** — when no newsletter carries numbers by T-1h, auto-populate Street consensus (Finnhub consensus already on the event) as a bogey row LABELED "sell-side consensus" — never presented as whisper/buyside. Precedent: RBRK 8/27 manual Zacks row. Goal: there is always something to measure against at print time.
  - (f) **Recap timing decision revisit** — user recalls deciding AMC recaps move to T+1 morning ("that night there's too much missing"); current behavior sent NVDA/CRWD/RBRK individual recaps same-evening ~15 min post-promotion (the AMC cluster wrap-pending gate did not engage). Decide: all-AMC-defer vs cluster-only, then align `earnings-pipeline.md`.

  - **(a) Preview printout = ONE sheet, email-exact bogeys — SHIPPED 2026-08-07 (`c026c4e` + `56ad89f`).** New `lib/earnings/print-sheet.ts` (`composePrintSheetHtml` + verbatim `extractBogiesTableMarkdown`) renders the LOCAL preview email's own HTML (scoreboard → sheet-bogeys-by-source → bogies table lifted byte-for-byte from `ai_output_md` → full user notes → past prints) through `briefingToHtml`; `lib/earnings/print-pdf.ts` (`renderHtmlToPdf` — `%%EOF`-gated poll for completion, since headless Chrome never fires `close` on this Mac; DI spawn seam; `printPdfViaLp`) turns it into a PDF and prints it duplex (`sides=two-sided-long-edge`) so notes overflow lands on the physical back of the same sheet. `printWorksheetNow`/`printArmedWorksheets` try this road first and fall back to the monospace sheet on ANY failure (no Chrome, no local preview, render/print error — never-silent; a PDF-road `lp` failure downgrades to monospace AND stamps on its success rather than retrying stampless — ruling recorded 2026-08-07). **Same-day live-user feedback, fixed in `56ad89f`**: (1) the sheet printed the outbound email's amber/cream envelope palette verbatim — `PRINT_CSS` now forces every inline background/text the envelope emits to white/black under `@media print` with `!important` (table borders untouched — that ruled grid is the fillable surface; header cells keep a light-gray tint so the header row still reads); (2) a second page printed with only the footer note (its inline spacer + the ancestor table's `break-inside:avoid` pushed the whole block to an orphan page) — fixed by targeting the footer's unique inline-style substrings with a shrunk spacer + `break-before/inside:avoid`; (3) the one-sheet ladder gained a third rung — `{ compact: true }` re-render (smaller font/spacing) before the notes-never-truncate "print anyway" floor, capped at 3 renders total, never loops. Live-verified: white background renders pure white; fit ladder measured full=3pp → drop-past-prints=3pp → compact=2pp on a real BRK.A preview.
  - **(b) Post-bogeys prose quality + provenance walkthrough — SHIPPED 2026-08-07 (`c026c4e`).** Root cause diagnosed first (catalog: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-06-artifact-catalog.md`): `callClaude`'s `textBlocks.map(b => b.text).join("\n")` planted a bare mid-sentence newline at every `web_search` citation boundary — confirmed in 6/6 sampled sends — which the HTML renderer then split into disjoint one-line paragraphs. Fixed at the source (`joinClaudeTextBlocks`, joins with `""` not `"\n"`) rather than patching the renderer — a renderer-side fix was tried and rejected because it also merged genuine paragraph breaks (25→16 paragraphs on real data). A read-boundary repair (`lib/earnings/repair-citation-linebreaks.ts`) covers already-stored emails, applied ONLY at the two earnings display read sites (`/api/earnings/email-content` + `BogeysEditModal`) — never at compose, since compose is fixed at the root. New deterministic `renderSheetBogeysBlock` (`lib/digest/send-earnings-email.ts`, code-built from `earnings_bogeys`, zero AI) gives conflicting sheet-source bogeys (e.g. TMT Breakout vs FundaAI) their own per-source columns in both preview and recap instead of forcing a merge. Prompt rules: never re-list the system-rendered sheet-bogeys table, cite source labels in-cell when using sheet values, show disagreeing sources side-by-side rather than merged, and 1–3 flowing paragraphs per section (no filler openers, no orphan bold fragments).
  - **(c) User stock notes get cut off in printouts — SHIPPED 2026-08-07 (`c026c4e`).** Notes-are-sacred now holds on the primary print roads: the PDF road renders every family note in full (no cap, no chop); the monospace fallback's silent 4-notes×74-char chop is retired in favor of full-text word-wrapping, still bounded by the existing page cap. (The no-local-preview deterministic composer wasn't touched this round — see the deferred-minors item below.)
  - **(d) Post-print sequence → LIVE PRINT-WATCH — brainstormed + spec'd 2026-08-20.** Reframed from "fewer manual touchpoints" to "the app is the print-time surface": bogey sheet fills live within seconds of the wire. STILL USER-RUN: import September statements (DONE 2026-10-07; rehearsal on a copy clean), then rehearse + run `recompute-tax-lots-v2.ts --apply --live` (still owed). Follow-ups: (a) the statement pass dates a confirmation at the account's LATEST statement date, so a flat omitted by an earlier statement lands later (can cross a tax year at Dec/Jan); (b) undoing a batch that owns a relabelled same-date tombstone deletes it and the pair reads held until the next live reconcile; (c) QA's tax-lots Recompute finding should judge idempotence as press-twice-identical. — Recompute drift ROOT-CAUSED 2026-10-02 — not non-idempotence** (read-only, real-figure report `docs/private/recompute-diagnosis-2026-10-02.md`). Two presses on one copy are identical on source_key digests; the nightly "11→9" is STORED (computed 09-15, stamp v3:103) vs ENGINE on newer inputs (generation 159): every bump since is a live TWS/Plaid sync, and the broker-close pass in `lib/compute/tax-lots.ts` (~990-1120, newest-holdings `MAX(as_of_date)` + `quantity = 0`) mints a persisted RECONCILE_CLOSE whenever the newest LIVE snapshot shows a name flat, priced at that snapshot day. Defects: live snapshots are positions, not trades (one synthetic close stands in for several real fills); partial reductions get no close while full exits do; the close's date/price/source_key move every time a name round-trips; the "current" stamp depends on live rows. Options: (A) only statement-sourced zero rows (`holding-sources.ts` statement class, `:stmt` tombstones) mint persisted closes; live-only flats render as "pending statement" and stay out of realized / 8949 / broker reconciliation (recommended); (B) leave as is (drift continues nightly). Also: QA should test idempotence as press-twice-identical and report stored-vs-engine as "stale since generation N". USER-RUN either way: import the September IBKR activity + both September Vanguard statements (not on disk yet), then rehearse `recompute-tax-lots-v2.ts --apply --verify-idempotent` on a copy and run `--apply --live`. Broker roll-up matcher and opening-lots importer are NOT blocked by this and can be built now.

The confidence drop after a press is the position-vs-lot integrity scan waking up (it is DARK while the convention stamp is stale and lights when the press re-stamps), not new damage; the minted synthetic closes and the unmatched-close warnings come from historical IBKR fills carrying no open/close direction note, so every short round-trip fails to match under the chronological engine. Pressing Recompute BEFORE the direction backfill would publish phantom realized gains (synthetic closes of un-covered cover lots); with the backfill rehearsed on a copy the synthetic closes and unmatched warnings collapse to a handful, short lots are modeled, and realized figures sit next to the legacy ones. - [ ] **[tax] Bond accrued interest lands inside lot cost basis and proceeds** (2026-09-04) — the canonical convention is one BUY/SELL row whose `amount` carries the full dirty cash and whose `price` is clean, and `netLegDollars` takes the lot dollars from `amount`, so every bond lot's basis and every bond sale's proceeds are off by the accrued leg (the August Treasury sale's engine realized loss differs from the statement's for exactly this reason). Tax exports are already marker-gated NOT-FOR-FILING, so this is not user-visible as filing data. Decide: split accrued into its own INTEREST row at import, or net it out inside `netLegDollars` for `security_type = 'Bond'`. Medium; needs a decision before code.

- [x] ✅ **[slice A follow-up] Armed projection date horizon — DONE by the slice A fix wave (R23)**: `buildArmedEventsEntries` publishes LIVE entries only for armed events dated `>= today − 14`, so the payload stops growing as never-disarmed worksheets accumulate. An event that ages past the horizon drops out and is deliberately NOT tombstoned (it is still armed; nothing in the cloud selects an event that old, and a tombstone would be re-carried for 48h for nothing). The sweep-tick reconcile writes the first post-horizon generation naturally. Documented in `docs/reference/cron-and-workers.md` §15 + `docs/reference/earnings-pipeline.md`.
- [x] ✅ **[slice A follow-up] Prepare pass whole-pass budget — DONE by the slice A fix wave (F3)**: `PREPARE_PASS_BUDGET_MS` (5 min) is checked between rows, so N hung rows × 4 minutes can no longer run the 15-minute sweep tick into the next one; rows the budget never reached stay pending and unclaimed for the next tick, and the report carries `budgetExhausted`. The same wave moved the prepare + transcript passes AFTER the Mac-aliveness marker so neither can delay it.
- [x] ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-10-05 (`671fb8c3`, `pruneSentCloudOutbox`) — [slice A follow-up] Prune sent `cloud_outbox` rows** — nothing deletes an `armed-events` row once `sent_at` is stamped, so the table grows one row per projection change forever. Wanted: a periodic prune of rows with `sent_at IS NOT NULL` older than ~30 days that ALWAYS keeps the newest row of the kind (`readPreviousArmedEntries` reads it for the tombstone carry-forward — deleting it would re-publish every tombstone). Unsent rows are never pruned: they are the retry queue.
- [x] ✅ **[slice A follow-up, user-run] One-time disarm of past worksheets — DONE 2026-09-03 09:12 ET** — `earnings_worksheet_flags` rows are never cleared once a print passes, so Today still shows old prints as armed while the cloud (R23 horizon) no longer carries them. One pass disarming flags whose event is more than ~14 days old would make the two agree. Cosmetic until then; do it by hand and watch one sweep tick mint the reconciled generation. *(Result:)* 14 flags (08-05/08-06 prints) deleted by hand, 8 remain inside the horizon; the next sweep tick minted NO generation (`outboxReconciled:false`, outbox still at generation 1) — the correct D10 no-op, because R23 had already kept those events out of the payload. The original expectation that a tick would mint a reconciled generation was wrong.

- [ ] **Valuation engine: cash_balance is 0 on daily rows before the first RESOLVABLE monthly anchor** (root-caused 2026-09-02 from the equity-curve base-day finding — the fixer's display floor only hides it on the curve): `lib/compute/daily-valuation.ts` Phase 1 writes every day with cash 0, Phase 2 attaches cash by stepping FORWARD from each monthly anchor, and an anchor with no priced daily row inside its tolerance is skipped — so daily rows that fall in a skipped window (the taxable account's first two daily rows at the 2026-03 prices-gap edge, just before the month-end anchor) keep cash 0 while holdings are complete — a large fake step into the anchor day that every daily_valuations consumer sees. Fix = back-step: carry the first resolvable anchor's cash residual BACKWARD over those days (reverse the flow stepping; seam-aware), or refuse to emit pre-first-resolvable-anchor rows; prove with a synthetic series (statement anchor N, no daily row at N, priced rows at N+27/N+30/N+31). Never a row repair — the engine regenerates these on every recompute. **PARTLY SHIPPED 2026-10-05 (`163bf9e1`):** the window between a skipped anchor and the first resolvable anchor is now back-stepped through recorded flows (seam-aware, in-kind legs excluded). STILL OPEN: (i) rows before the account's very FIRST anchor still carry cash 0 — an existing test pins that behaviour, needs a ruling; (ii) a skipped anchor in the MIDDLE of the series still leaves its window at 0.

Design nuance that blocks a copy-paste: reconcile's stale rows stay archived on the superseded row, but a correction DELETES its doomed rows, so "don't repoint" = CASCADE-destroying the archived email prose (the exact bug `f5c5b74` fixed). Fix shape needs a decision: park stale previews on a tombstone, or make the send road tolerate/replace a stale preview row (UNIQUE(event_id,phase) upsert) instead of gating at the writer. Only bites on a cross-window date correction where a preview already went out — rare; small once the shape is picked.
- [ ] **Import dedupe silently no-ops changed values — add a same-key-different-values conflict warning** (found 2026-08-10 during the December forensics): the deterministic `source_key` dedupe treats "same key again" and "same key, DIFFERENT values" identically — both skip. That's how batch 26's wrong December draft rows survived the corrected re-import (batch 35) and poisoned the TWR headline for months. Fix shape: at import commit, when a row's source_key already exists AND the incoming values differ beyond tolerance, keep the skip (idempotence stays) but surface a conflict count in the import preview/result + log the diffs — the user decides whether to run a repair. Applies to snapshot-family imports first (`monthly_snapshots`); audit which other importers share the pattern. Small-medium.

Residual: LQDT 8/06 (stored 44.84) had no daily bar to repair from — re-run the script after an ohlcv backfill covers it; foreign-listing (402340) session-clock nuance deliberately out of scope (US-RTH keying still improved it). **[Follow-ups closed 2026-08-10 evening: (a) betas re-run clean — 67 computed, 0 errors, no locks; (b) post-repair valuations recompute confirmed in server.log (20:35Z sync, `valuationsRecomputed:true`); ETF-types check: all nine still 'ETF' after a full TWS sync — guard holding. - [x] **Earnings coverage-gap push notification listed only ETFs — ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED + REPAIRED 2026-08-10** (`14f0b34`; user report same day; the push fired Sat 8/09 16:47 ET from the briefing road's guard). The guard's filters were CORRECT — the data lied: securities rows for ARKK/HACK/IGV/KRE/SOXX/SPY/TLT/UCO/NCLD carried `security_type='Stock'` because IBKR labels every STK contract a stock (TWS positions map at `lib/tws/positions.ts` + ibkr-activity parser), defeating both the guard's stock-only candidate filter AND its 7/16 option-underlying ETF exclusion. Three-layer fix (each alone shown insufficient live): (1) enrich reads contract-details `stockType` and promotes NULL/'Stock'→'ETF' (ETF/ETN only, never downgrades); (2) `upsertSecurity` conflict clause treats incoming 'Stock' as weak evidence — never downgrades a fund-family type (the first live retype was reverted WITHIN MINUTES by the running app's TWS sync through the old COALESCE — caught and guarded same session); (3) `scripts/repair-etf-types.ts` applied live for all nine (backup taken, verified stuck post-guard). Remaining legit gaps (BANC, ZS, BRK/B) are real stocks, correctly flagged.

Downgrade to watch; close after 2-3 more clean DMG builds.)* — `electron-builder --mac` builds and SIGNS `dist/mac-arm64/Vanguard Dashboard.app` fine, then dies in `dmgbuild` writing `/Volumes/Vanguard Dashboard/.DS_Store` (Errno 28) even with 32 GiB free on `/` — the staging volume is sized too small for the app, not a disk-space problem. **Workaround used + verified this session: `npm run electron:install` alone** (it copies the already-built, already-signed `.app` to `/Applications` and relaunches — no DMG involved), so local deploys are unaffected; only the distributable `.dmg` is missing. Fix candidates: bump the dmg `format`/size in `electron-builder.yml`, or drop DMG packaging entirely for local-only distribution (`--dir`). Small; only blocks producing a shareable DMG. **(2026-08-11: two more clean notarized DMG builds — morning node@24 migration + session-end. That is 3 consecutive clean; CLOSING as transient per the close-rule. Reopen if it recurs.)**
- [x] **`electron:install` relaunches the WRONG app — FIXED 2026-08-10** (`b21fbad`): both the `osascript ... to quit` and the `open` in the `electron:install` script now use the `/Applications/Vanguard Dashboard.app` full-path form, exactly as prescribed below. *(Original report, hit live 2026-08-09 during the send-race deploy: Two registered apps share the name: the freshly-installed `/Applications/Vanguard Dashboard.app` and the stay-open Desktop launcher `~/Desktop/Vanguard Dashboard.app`. LaunchServices picked the Desktop one, which runs `scripts/launch-dashboard.sh` → `npm run dev`, so the deploy ended with a **dev server on :3099 and the packaged app not running at all** — silently, since both answer :3099 and both serve the same source. Resolution order isn't stable, which is why prior deploys worked. Recovery used this session: quit the launcher by full path (`osascript -e 'tell application "/Users/Yitzi/Desktop/Vanguard Dashboard.app" to quit'`), then `open "/Applications/Vanguard Dashboard.app"` (path form, unambiguous). Fix is one word in the `electron:install` script — `open -a 'Vanguard Dashboard'` → `open "/Applications/Vanguard Dashboard.app"`; the sibling `osascript ... to quit` at the head of that script is ambiguous the same way and should also take the full path. Trivial, but it silently downgrades every deploy it hits.)*

- [x] **Earnings newsletter fill — single-ranked-source SQL flood hardening — FIXED 2026-07-20 evening** (final-review Minor j, 2026-07-17). Went with the window-function option: `PER_SOURCE_FETCH_CAP = MAX_NEWSLETTER_ARTICLES × 3` (=18 — derived so a genuinely single-source symbol still yields 6 usable post-supersedence articles for pass-2) via `ROW_NUMBER() PARTITION BY source_id` before the rank-aware `LIMIT 30`. Because 18 < 30, a capped flooder leaves ≥12 pool slots for other sources AND `distinctSources` becomes truthful for free (1-source pool now implies only one source covers the family) — no separate `COUNT(DISTINCT)` query needed. Regression test watched-fail first (6× flooder, others evicted).

- ✅ **What-if silent unknown-symbol drop** — root cause: `applyLegs` (lib/compute/exposure-delta.ts) `continue`d past unknown/unheld legs and the `ExposureDelta` payload had no field to report it (info destroyed server-side). - ✅ **"+ Note" orphaned journal notes (4th sighting)** — root cause found for real this time: TWO add-note links on Security Detail; the top "+ Note" emitted `?security=<id>` which the composer never reads (it prefills from `?type=`+`?symbol=` only; `security` only filters the list), while the working in-section link only renders when notes already exist — which is exactly why 3 prior closures were non-repro flips. Fix: top href now `view=notes&type=trade_thesis&symbol=<sym>&security=<id>`.
- ✅ **Performance frozen risk-metrics** — NOT a wiring bug: `startDate` threads correctly, but drawdown/Sharpe/curve read `daily_valuations` (floor 2026-03-27; risk full-coverage floor ~04-06) while TWR reads multi-year `monthly_snapshots`, so 3Y/All select identical rows. Fix (user-chosen honest labeling): `computeRiskMetrics` exposes `seriesStart/seriesEnd`; new pure `lib/compute/data-window.ts::dataWindowNotice()` (7-day grace, reuses `addDays`) captions the KPI strip + equity curve with the actual daily-data window. Self-heals as history accrues.

### Closed this session (2026-07-21 evening — TWS session-eviction root-cause + polite-yield fix, then probe-driven pivot to sessionless holdings)

- ✅ **"TWS keeps logging me out" ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED, then PIVOTED on scenario-b probe evidence** (branch `ibkr-session-yield`, a690fca..6522470, 11 commits, subagent-driven 6 tasks + reviews — MERGED to main ff + pushed, Worker deployed `5a7c6e8d`, Electron rebuilt, all 2026-07-21). Root cause: IBKR allows ONE brokerage session per username and every Web API touch POSTed `ssodh/init` with `compete:"true"`, deliberately evicting the TWS desktop login — fired by earnings-intel straddle passes (cockpit polling ~30-min cadence during earnings season), the disconnected-path refresh (kick → socket drop → 30-min re-kick feedback loop), and Worker earnings ticks that fetched live IBKR BEFORE marker checks. First fix (Tasks 1-5) shipped a blanket `compete:"false"` design. The mandatory scenario-b probe (TWS closed, required before merge) then DISPROVED that design: `compete:"false"` is refused in BOTH TWS-open AND TWS-closed runs (`auth/status` fails with `"Force compete capability must be used together with compete flag"` — this consumer key is provisioned with the force-compete capability and can never use `compete:"false"`) — a blanket compete:"false" design would have broken every away-from-desk Web API path. **Task 8 pivot (probe-driven):** holdings reads go SESSIONLESS — `/portfolio/accounts`, `/portfolio/{acct}/positions/{page}`, `/portfolio/{acct}/ledger` all return full data on a signed LST alone in every scenario (probe row 4), so Mac `fetchIbkrPortfolio` and Worker `fetchLiveIbkrPositions` no longer call `ssodh/init` at all — zero eviction risk, works with TWS open or closed. Only `/iserver`-needing consumers (earnings-intel Web API road, quote enrichment) still call `openSession`, which now ALWAYS uses `compete:"true"` (the only value this key accepts) gated behind a new `isTwsListeningLocally` check — a raw TCP probe of the local TWS API port, run BEFORE any IBKR network call; if TWS is listening locally, yield immediately (no LST mint, no IBKR call); only when TWS is confirmed absent does `compete:"true"` reach IBKR, with nothing to evict. `isSessionYield`'s post-init check is now a defensive backstop only. Worker's local `isSessionYield` mirror deleted (import-dead — the Worker holdings path is sessionless). `IbkrSessionYieldError` / `"ibkr-session-yield"` sentinel, degrade paths (intel falls to the next road, Worker's yield catch in `fallback-earnings.ts` is now defensive/unreachable, refresh releases the sync mutex), and the **TWS-first straddle road** (`lib/tws/atm-straddle.ts`) all carry over unchanged from Tasks 1-5 — only the yield DETECTION moved from the IBKR response body to the local port check. Single-machine TWS assumption documented. Docs updated: `scripts/probe-ibkr-compete.ts` header (full four-row verdict table), root CLAUDE.md + `lib/tws/CLAUDE.md` (compete bullets rewritten), `scripts/ibkr-refresh-holdings.ts` header. Tests 3757 Mac (root suite, includes Worker files) + 431 Worker (package-local) at head, both tsc clean.

**Live watches / follow-ups filed from this session:** first live successful `openSession` (compete:"true") call with TWS confirmed closed via the local port gate, verifying `/iserver` reads work away from the desk; no TWS logout during a full trading day post-deploy with TWS open (the gate should short-circuit every attempt before any IBKR call); first live "straddle via TWS" log line during an earnings pass with TWS open; Worker `wrangler tail` confirming zero `ssodh/init` calls from the Worker's live-IBKR fetch path ever, not just on marker-skipped ticks.

## Closed this session (2026-08-09 — Mac-first send race)

> User asked why the Sunday briefing had not arrived; it had — twice. The Worker cloud fallback shipped a thin copy at 16:47 ET and the Mac shipped its full copy at 16:51, with byte-identical subject lines. Root-caused to TWO independent defects (either sufficient alone to lose the race), fixed both, mutation-verified both regression pins. Suite 4,457 Mac + 457 Worker green, tsc clean.

- ✅ **Mac-first send race FIXED** (branch `mac-first-send-race`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-09-mac-first-send-race-design.md` @ `7c7dd52`, implementation `1bc39e1`). **Defect 1**: the cron routes fired `void setRunningMarker(type)` and immediately entered the pipeline; synchronous better-sqlite3 work held the event loop for 215.6s during auto-refresh, so the un-awaited fetch never got a turn and its own 3s AbortController killed it — the marker was never written, and the failure surfaced ~7 min later as a misleading "worker unreachable" warning. The awaited `checkCloudMarker` one line earlier succeeded on the same tick, which is how the network was ruled out. **Defect 2**: `RUNNING_TTL_SECONDS` was 10 min — shorter than the gap from every Mac tick to the Worker's dispatch (digest 8:45→expiry 8:55 vs Worker 9:00; evening 19:00→19:10 vs 19:15; briefing 16:30→16:40 vs 16:45), so **digest and evening were never covered at all** and the briefing only when its launchd tick slipped past 16:35. Fast pipelines had masked both by winning on `mac-sent` instead (8/02 finished 1m54s early). **Fix**: `withRunningMarker` awaits the initial set and heartbeats every 2 min for the lifetime of the send (so no constant has to predict pipeline length again — the TTL now only has to outlive one starved gap), TTL raised 10→15 min, and `confirmMacSent` moved inside the wrapper and awaited, closing a pre-existing sub-second window where a fire-and-forget clear could land before the mac-sent write and leave neither marker set.
- ✅ **Scope decisions recorded**: briefing/digest/evening only (earnings recaps stay deliberately un-gated per 2026-08-05); heartbeat + 15-min grace over a single long TTL; no subject labeling and the Mac never suppresses its own send — suppressing on a cloud-sent marker would have meant receiving ONLY the degraded copy on 8/09, inverting the goal.
- ✅ **Both regression pins mutation-verified** (not just red-green on a missing symbol): removing the heartbeat fails only the renewal test (`expected 1 to be >= 7`); reverting the `await` to `void` fails only the ordering test. Each pin catches its own defect and nothing else.
- ✅ **Deployed**: Worker `npx wrangler deploy` (version `4eb0b0cf`), branch pushed, CLAUDE.md's Workers-Cron-hybrid entry corrected (it still documented the fire-and-forget 10-min behavior).
- New open item filed: the earnings sweep's running marker carries the same `void` + 10-min-TTL shape at lower severity (see Bugs / Quality).
- **Live verification pending**: Monday 8:45 ET digest is a free end-to-end check that sends nothing extra — if `mac-running-digest-<date>` is still in KV at 9:00 (where it would previously have expired at 8:55), the fix is confirmed on real traffic.

### Closed this session (2026-09-04 evening — print-v2 B/C/D landing, 089 incident, test seatbelt)

- ✅ **Slices B, C and D all merged to `main` and the app rebuilt.** `ff37c881` (B, clean), `51fa458e` (C), and D rebased onto C and merged. Final merged tree: **684 test files, 8,217 passed, 0 failed**; `next build` clean; `tsc --noEmit` at its documented baseline of 20 errors in four test files this work never touches. Three rebase conflicts, all resolved by keeping both sides: `PrintStatusEntry` carries C's window/go fields AND D's read/callout fields (C's hunk also opened `GoRequestSummary`, so the brace structure needed care); `docs/DECISIONS.md` keeps both slice sections; and `lib/print-watch/register.ts` now runs **both** C's go handler and D's first-pass handler ahead of B's — `print_watch_go_requests`, `print_watch_reads` and `print_watch_callouts` all reference `print_watch_prints` with no cascade, so each must repoint its own rows before B deletes the donor print. C before D only matches slice order: verified no foreign key runs between the go tables and the first-pass tables.
- ✅ **R-C7's test asserted a handler CENSUS, not an ordering** (`e5250c60`) — the one failure the merged suite caught. C pinned `listEventMergeHandlers()` and the merge report's handler list as exact arrays, written when C's was the only handler ahead of B's; D's sibling test in `first-pass-merge.test.ts` asserted the same invariant by relative index and survived untouched. Both of C's now assert position, so slices E and F cannot break them for a reason unrelated to the go-row FK.
- ✅ **[INCIDENT] Migration 089 applied itself to the LIVE database from the test suite** (2026-09-04 16:33:59 ET). Merging slice B put `089_print_watch_document_identity.ts` into the default `CODE_MIGRATIONS` registry; `lib/db.ts` runs `runMigrations(db)` at MODULE LOAD against `resolveDbPath()`, which with no `DATABASE_PATH` is `<cwd>/data/vanguard.db`. The first full-suite run after the merge therefore applied 089 to the real database 20 seconds in, bypassing `scripts/migrate-089-document-identity.ts` and every gate it exists to enforce. **It went unnoticed through 7,899 tests, 8/8 E2E and three Codex rounds because every slice was verified in a WORKTREE, where `data/vanguard.db` does not exist — the same code path silently creates a throwaway DB and migrates that.** All three slice worktrees hold one (976K–996K). ACCEPTED by user ruling rather than rolled back: the end state is the one `--live` would have produced and was independently verified — the migration's own candidate-conservation invariant ran and passed (it throws otherwise, and the transaction committed), `integrity_check` ok, `foreign_key_check` clean, and all 9 surviving documents have their bytes on disk, so the skipped bytes gate would have passed too. Genuinely lost: a pre-089 backup (none was taken) and the human-acknowledged report. Post-incident backup at `data/backups/post-089-incident-2026-09-04T20-36-10Z.db`.
- ✅ **`next build` opened the live database too — the SECOND door of the same class, now closed.** Found by chasing a timestamp that did not fit: migrations stamped 20:50:20 while the server only started at 20:59:06. Next evaluates route modules while collecting page data, those reach `lib/db.ts`, and it migrates at module load — so the packaged-app deploy chain's BUILD step, not the app launch, is what applied 090 and 091, nine minutes before the new binary was installed (the OLD binary ran against the NEW schema for that window). `npm run build` now pins `DATABASE_PATH=":memory:"`, with `tests/repo/build-never-touches-live-db.test.ts` pinning it for any script invoking `next build`. **In-memory rather than a scratch FILE on purpose:** Next collects page data with SEVEN parallel workers that all open whatever the path names — pointed at a shared fresh file they race each other's migrations and the build dies with `table accounts already exists` (observed, exit 1). That race was always there; it never fired because the live DB already had every migration applied, so `runMigrations` was a no-op. Residual, documented in the test: a bare `npx next build` typed by hand still resolves to the live database. Verified: build exit 0, 103/103 static pages, nothing created on disk, live `schema_migrations` unchanged at 91 across the build.
- ✅ **Seatbelt: tests can never open the live database** (`4da7daa3`). `tests/setup/db-guard.ts` (new vitest `setupFile`) pins `DATABASE_PATH` at a per-worker-PROCESS scratch file before any test module loads, so parallel workers never race one file; an explicitly-set `DATABASE_PATH` is still honoured (the QA sandbox recipe uses one) unless it IS the live database, tested by realpath plus `(dev, ino)` — the same identity check the 089 runner uses — so a symlink or hardlink spelling cannot slip past. `tests/repo/tests-never-touch-live-db.test.ts` asserts the invariant itself (where the database RESOLVES) rather than banning an import, because the incident came through a TRANSITIVE import: all four tests that name `@/lib/db` mock it. Its third case loads the real unmocked singleton, so the seal is exercised on every suite run. Verified: a real singleton import opens the scratch DB and leaves the live file's mtime unchanged; absolute, relative and symlink spellings of the live path are all refused. Every full-suite run since left `data/vanguard.db` untouched.
